## Future-proof care

<!-- image -->

## Ambea - the competency-based company

Ambea is one of the leading care providers in Scandinavia. We work with the elderly, and people with disabilities or a need for psychosocial support. Our mission is to ensure quality of life for every person in Ambea's approximately 950 units. Seeing and hearing them is the heart of our company.

But we also look up and see the world through the eyes of our clients - municipalities. Will they have enough resources in the years ahead? As care needs grow sharply, financial pressures intensify and the care worker shortage deepens, smart solutions, partnerships and innovative solutions will be crucial. Ambea has a key role to play here.

We are a company that dares to test new ideas, strives for continuous improvement and continuously develops our units and our employees. We are big enough to make a difference and want to be a role model that works together with municipalities to create as much safe and secure care as possible from every valuable tax krona.

Always in the best interests of our care receivers. Always guided by our vision: We make the world a better place, one person at a time.

## Ambea in brief

Ambea works to create a good life for every care receiver in our units across Scandinavia. The operations were founded more than 100 years ago and the company has been listed on Nasdaq Stockholm since 2017.

## 31,000 employees

950 units

16,500 care receivers

450 municipalities are our clients

<!-- image -->

## OUR SERVICES

- Elderly care: At Ambea's just over 100 nursing homes, our care receivers live in their own apartments with access to communal areas, activities and mealtimes. Staff are on duty 24 hours a day. We also offer home care in selected locations across Sweden.
- Disability care: For people with permanent disabilities, Ambea has care services for all needs and stages of life: from child care homes and schools to group homes, assisted living facilities and day services for adults. Personal assistance is also offered in Norway.
- Psychosocial support: Ambea offers support for people with psychosocial problems, such as substance abuse, self-harming behaviour or criminality. These initiatives include HVB homes, assisted living facilities, foster homes, sheltered housing and non-residential care.
- Competence and staffing solutions: Ambea offers flexible and effective solutions for welfare service staffing.
- Competence development: Ambea's training company, Lära, provides training and competence-enhancement programmes in social work, care, schools and treatment - both internally and externally.

## CLIENTS AND CONTRACTS

Ambea's clients are municipalities across Scandinavia that purchase care for their residents. The services can be divided according to contractual form:

- Own Management: Care placements under Own Management operations are offered in premises that Ambea rents with longterm contracts, usually 10-15 years. Due to the long duration, the premises can be tailored to Ambea's working methods and concepts. Municipalities purchase placements according to need.
- Contract Management: Contract Management means that Ambea takes over an existing care service in the municipality's premises and runs the unit for a specific period of time, usually four to five years, often with a possible extension of two to four years.
- Staffing and training: Separate services, or subscriptions and framework agreements. Customers include municipalities, regions and individual companies.

Control and reporting

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

## The year in brief

-  In 2022, Ambea posted its highest-ever net sales and adjusted EBITA.
-  Ambea continues to grow, and acquired five units and opened ten new residential facilities during the year with 409 care placements.
-  Ambea launched its updated strategy for future-proof care. The aim of the strategy is to meet the growing need for care, strengthen Ambea's market position and create long-term growth.
-  In line with Ambea's strategy, parts of elderly care in Stendi were divested and Klara divest its staffing solutions for doctors in order to focus on subscription services and specialised solutions.
-  Due to the strong financial situation, the Board decided to launch a share buyback programme prior to the 2023 Annual General Meeting.
-  For the second consecutive year, Ambea was included on Universum' top 50 list of Sweden's Most Attractive Employers.
-  To reduce our carbon footprint, Ambea prioritises the phase-out of fossil-fuel vehicles. Electric cars were ordered during the year and in 2023, they will replace around 40 per cent of cars that run on fossil fuel.
-  In 2022, 7,000 employees in Sweden completed one of Lära's training courses.
-  Due to the war in Ukraine, both Ambea and our employees have made donations to The Red Cross, SOS Barnbyar and UNHCR, which have operations across Ukraine and in neighbouring countries. Ambea's purchasing processes were reviewed to ensure we are not purchasing from suppliers that are active in Russia. In Sweden, we have employed around 30 Ukrainian refugees.

CEO INTERVIEW

## Clear path forward with updated strategy

In an interview, Ambea's CEO Mark Jensen summarises some of the key events of the year and looks ahead.

## How would you summarise 2022 for Ambea?

A stable year with positive growth despite a turbulent business environment. Ambea achieved its highest-ever sales during the year, which meant we could provide even more care receivers with support and assistance. Demand for elderly care and social care was favourable in Sweden, and Vardaga and Nytida showed a positive trend during the year. In Stendi, we worked with capacity adjustments and ensuring that we have the right staffing, which began to show results in the second half of the year. In 2023, we will continue working to strengthen profitability in Stendi. In Denmark and Altiden, the focus was on the integration of several acquisitions and creation of a stable and sustainable platform for future growth. In our smallest division, Klara, we completed a strategic transition during the year with both acquisitions and divestments, which will create even better opportunities moving forward.

## Was there anything in particular during the year that you would like to mention?

All of my meetings with care receivers and employees. I prioritise making many visits to our units and everywhere that I go, I meet highly knowledgeable and committed employees. My experience is that we are always striving to fulfil our vi -sion - to make the world a better place, one person at a time. That means that we are always moving forward and developing our business at all levels. It is also clear that demands on

<!-- image -->

'Ambea can make a contribution and help our clients with smart and efficient solutions so that society can continue to deliver personalised care and more people can benefit from this care.'

us are increasing, so I would like to highlight our systematic quality management. In addition, I would like to thank all of our employees for their value-creating efforts during the year.

## How have external events affected Ambea?

In many different ways. The Russian invasion of Ukraine has caused human suffering, as we all know, and we have tried to support the Ukrainian people and society in various ways. We have organised collections and donated funds to support humanitarian aid in the country. In Sweden, we have provided properties for refugee accommodation and created work experience placements and jobs for Ukrainians who have fled the war. Our units have also organised races and activities to collect clothing, food and hygiene kits that have then been

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

sent to Ukraine. I am really proud of the commitment and local initiatives that have been taken within the organisation. And like everyone else, we've been affected by the economic situation with high inflation and sharply rising prices for food and energy. Despite the fact that we acted fast and took measures to dampen the effect, our costs rose to more than SEK 100 million for the full-year.

## Have you updated your strategy?

Yes. It was a good time to update the strategy to move Ambea forward, and to take advantage of the business opportunities that we see in the coming years. All Scandinavian countries are facing a sharp increase in the need for care due to a growing and ageing population. An ageing population will not only increase the need for care, but also place strain on municipal budgets. Ambea can make a contribution here and help our clients with smart and efficient solu -tions so that society can continue to deliver personalised care and more people can benefit from this care.

## Can you tell us about the strategy, and what is new?

We call it the 'Strategy for future-proof care.' The message we want to send is that we have solutions to the welfare challenges ahead, and that we are contributing to sustainable development. We have also identified and will work with four strategic focus area and the important dimension of sustainability. We have clarified our position - we want to be the leading care company in the markets in which we operate. That means that we will continue to focus on development and profitable growth in the segments where we are strong, but exclude areas with low development potential such as elderly care in Norway or home care in Denmark. In addition, we will place extra focus on the development of services based on the needs of our care receivers and clients, and our relentless work with competence enhancement and quality improvement will continue. We also want to create more time for our employees to deliver care by eliminating all unnecessary administration and increasing innovation in social welfare. We have an exciting future ahead of us, and Ambea can offer solutions that create sustainable care for all. Read more about the strategy here.

## What are your predictions for 2023?

We will continue to deliver good and high-quality care to a growing number of care receivers. In 2023, there will be slightly fewer new-starts year-on-year, but there will be more in 2024, so we will also be preparing for them. In addition, we will be focused on implementing the strategy, actively seeking compatible acquisitions and working with business leadership in the day-to-day operations. We will always be affected by the world around us and the economic situation, but that doesn't have to be all negative. As municipal budgets become more and more strained, the interest in private alternatives increases because we offer knowledge, quality and cost efficiency. At the same time, elderly care must be sig -nificantly expanded in Sweden and Denmark. So I would like to see more political dialogue and collaboration around how we create new care homes. This is becoming more and more urgent because the number of elderly people and people with dementia will grow sharply over the next five to six years.

## Do you have any suggestions for how the expansion could be sped up?

Yes. Two specific recommendations that Ambea supports are the introduction of a national freedom of choice reform in all municipalities through the Swedish Freedom of Choice Act (LOV) for example. That would provide clear and stable conditions for private and non-profit players to create more new care homes. It would also increase opportunities for people to choose a care home according to their own needs and wishes instead of being placed on a waiting list, which creates anxiety and reduces quality of life. To increase the rate of establishment in Denmark, where private and non-profit players are entitled to the same compensation as municipal operators, a uniform and transparent model is needed to calculate municipal costs something we would also like to see in Sweden.

## Some final words?

We are looking forward to the future, and to welcoming more care receivers to us 2023. The need for care will grow sharply in the coming years and we have an important role to play here. We are convinced that public and private sector collaboration will enable us to create the best solutions. I would also like to thank our clients for their confidence in us, our care receivers who spend their days with us, their loved ones for good cooperation and, not least, our employees for their invaluable contributions.

## ABOUT Mark Jensen

Mark Jensen has been Ambea's CEO since 2021. Prior to that, he was CEO and Nordic President of MTR Nordic AB. He has also spent ten years in senior positions at Carlsberg, including CEO of Carlsberg Sweden. Mark was born in 1971, and is originally from Denmark.

## Our strategy

Ambea is the leading care company in Scandinavia. We have a hundred-year history of developing social care, always based on the needs of our care receivers and always as a partner to the public sector. Due to a growing and ageing population, the need for care is expected to increase significantly in the coming years. That is creating new opportunities for Ambea and we are taking the next step in our development with our updated strategy for future-proof and sustainable care.

Based on society's needs, we will continue to invest in competence development, clear concepts and frameworks in our divisions. That creates the conditions for high-quality and personalised care that is also cost-efficient. We will also continue to invest in development and growth in the segments where we are strong, while excluding areas with low development potential.

Ambea has identified, and will work with, four focus areas:

- We offer care services with a customer focus.
- We deliver quality through competence.
- We create time for care.
- We accelerate innovation and welfare technology.

Alongside of our focus areas, sustainability plays a key role in our mission and we have prioritised five of the UN Sustainable Development Goals.

Read more about our sustainability practices on page 13

<!-- image -->

## STRATEGY FOR FUTURE-PROOF CARE

Our strategy goes under the name of Strategy for future-proof care . The strategy will guide us in the years ahead. It has no end date and we will keep it alive by monitoring our business environment, and then pressure testing and adjusting it every year.

Ambea's set of values Our World provides the basis for an organisation where everyone is moving in the same direction. It reminds our employees of why our company exists, the core of our mission and how we should respond to the world and each other.

<!-- image -->

## Care services with a customer focus

With the needs of our care receivers and clients as our starting point, we help society in the prioritised segments of elderly and social care.

- Customer knowledge and clear tender processes We have clear offerings that make it easy for our cli -ents and care receivers to make decisions. Knowing our customers well enables us to continuously develop our services and offer what they want, quickly and efficiently.
- Flexibility for meeting new requirements and expectations We are constantly developing the content of our services to meet the needs of both care receivers and clients.
- Growth that strengthens our business

Our core business is to develop residential care but we can also include related service areas when they strengthen our business and our core activities. We are organised so that we can seize opportunities and respond pro-actively to risks. We work systematically and use the entire organisation's knowledge to enable sustainable and structured growth.

<!-- image -->

## Klara is meeting new student health needs

During the year, our staffing division, Klara, grew in student health and can now offer nurses, school doctors, psychologists, counsellors, career advisors and special education teachers. This was due to Klara's acquisition of SkolPool, a market leader in student health services in Sweden.

The need for well-developed student health services is growing and the acquisition gave Klara a unique market position in Sweden in this niche. In line with Ambea's updated strategy, Klara divested its staffing solutions for doctors during the year, which had few synergies with Ambea's other activities, to focus on other services and specialised solutions instead, such as ambulatory care teams, student health and rehab teams. Due to the acquisition, Klara now offers both staffing services and total student health solutions with care provider responsibility to municipal and independent schools.

<!-- image -->

## We deliver quality through competence

The success of our company depends on our employees. By investing in competence development, language development and leadership based on a hands-on and active approach, we are raising levels of knowledge which, in turn, will lead to fewer discrepancies and higher customer satisfaction.

## • Lära training organisation

We build and develop competence within our own organi -sation as well as for other players. Our training organisation Lära plays a key role here. Lära helps us bring more qualified employees with the right competence into the sector, and ensure that our employees maintain effective and relevant levels of knowledge.

## • Strong employer brand and new recruitment bases

We want to be the first choice for students and new employees. That is why we are constantly developing new recruitment bases and working together with schools and employment agencies to create work experience placements and give more people an opportunity to enter the care sector.

## • The best leaders in the industry

We will continue to develop leadership based on an active and hands-on approach, with engaged employees who take responsibility for quality of care and our shared

- work environment.

## Competence development for strong leadership

<!-- image -->

Leadership and leadership development are a priority and Ambea's target is that at least seven of ten managers will be recruited internally.

In Norway, we conduct our leadership programmes in col -laboration with VID Specialised University. During the year, around 70 of Stendi's unit managers completed a tailored leadership programme at VID. The aim of the programme is to develop leadership skills and refresh subject knowledge in childcare and other health and social sciences.

The programme was created in close collaboration between Stendi and subject heads at VID, based on a need to meet toughened regulatory demands for formal qualifica -tions in these areas, while we are also working continuously to strengthen and update the professional skills of our employees. The programme focuses on strategy, values and the customer perspective at Ambea and Stendi.

Since 2016, all of our managers and leaders in Sweden have taken part in Lära's leadership programme with the aim of creating a common language and leadership approach. Via our training organisation Lära, there is a plan for every leader that provides opportunities for leadership development. In Denmark, a new leadership programme is under start-up.

'Through the programme, around 70 managers from Stendi have strengthened their skills in social care at managerial level this year.'

Hanne Løvli, Head of Human Resources, Stendi

<!-- image -->

## We create time for care

Our time is one of the most important things we can give to our care receivers. Our processes and tools help us free-up time for care and achieve consistently high quality.

## • Systematic quality management

Our starting point is that it should be easy for every employee and manager to do the right thing in any given situation. Our systematic quality management, combined with continuous learning, is fundamental to meeting the high quality requirements of our various customers.

## • Time for the right things

We review tools to ensure they support employees in their day-to-day work. Our processes are revised twice annually. Everyone should feel secure with our procedures and be able to devote time to the right things.

## • Systems that facilitate

We are developing a future digital workplace to reduce and facilitate administrative tasks. Examples include mobile systems that meet employees' needs.

<!-- image -->

## Doing the right thing is easy with service processes

Nytida's service processes support employees and describe what has to be done, when and by whom, from when we receive a placement request until when the person leaves and no longer needs our support or care.

During the year, Nytida spent a great deal of time updating, clarifying and visualising the service processes. The service processes ensure quality and provide reassurance that everybody is working the same way, and the employees know they are complying with the applicable laws and requirements.

'The service process is our way of working. By following the service process, the legal and regulatory requirements become clear.'

Niklas Lundin, Sector Manager LSS, Nytida

<!-- image -->

## We accelerate innovation and welfare technology

Technology is evolving fast and expectations from care receivers, loved ones and clients are changing. Welfare technology can contribute to better care and a better work environment for our employees, while also improving effi -ciency.

- Increase the pace of development in care We are innovative and always seeking partnerships to increase the pace of development in care.
- Make life easier and improve quality We seek innovative solutions that can help to improve quality and make life easier for both care receivers and employees.

## • Create new business

We want to accelerate innovation in the care sector and are developing innovative solutions by developing our core business, company acquisitions and strategic partnerships.

'Society is facing major demographic changes. Our new updated strategy is positioning us to meet social challenges, create value by delivering higher quality at lower cost, and to contribute with innovation, development and the freedom to choose in care.'

Yrjö Närhinen, Chair of Ambea's Board about the new strategy

<!-- image -->

## Digital solutions that make life easier

Our employees are highly motivated when it comes to technology that creates added value for our care receivers, or that frees up more time for care.

When a unit tests a new technology, it often stems from the needs of a specific person or department, and the solution then turns out to be useful for others. During the year, a survey of the various types of welfare technology used by our Swedish units was carried out. Two simple but appreciated solutions are PictureMyLife, a digital communication tool, and Tovertafel, a games console for people with dementia that stimulates physical activity and social interaction.

In 2023, the focus will lie on identifying solutions that generate the most value for care receivers and employees, and then broaden them to benefit the entire Group.

Vardaga Sveavägen Karlstad uses Tovertafel. The games console stimulates physical activity and social interaction for people with dementia.

<!-- image -->

## Sustainable care

Our mission is to create enough safe and sustainable care for everyone. Sustainable care is about taking active responsibility for our society, participating in the creation of solutions to future-proof care, and using every tax krona to contribute to lasting social values.

Our efforts to achieve future-proof and sustainable worldclass care are based on four perspectives:

- Social care, where we are a responsible provider of jobs and care in an inclusive world.
- Climate-smart care.
- A high-trust business with robust control.
- And giving clear value back to society.

We build and develop competence within our own organisation and for other players through Lära, our training organisation.

<!-- image -->

## WE PRIORITISE FIVE OF THE UN SUSTAINABLE DEVELOPMENT GOALS

## Good health and well-being

Our vision is to make the world a better place, one person at a time. Ambea offers high-quality social care for our care receivers and security for their loved ones. Our goal is to meet the personal needs and wishes of each individual.

<!-- image -->

## Quality Education

We use Lära, our internal and external training organisation, to train tomorrow's employees. We create opportunities for more people to increase their vocational skills, while also helping to raise knowledge about nursing and care throughout the community.

<!-- image -->

## Affordable and Clean Energy

By ensuring that Ambea's Own Management operations always use renewable energy, we are helping to increase overall market demand for renewable and fossil-free energy.

<!-- image -->

## Decent Work and Economic Growth

Ambea's operations begin with caring for our employees - when they are happy and healthy, they can do more for our care receivers. Ambea offers job and workplace security. We develop managers who listen and lead, and invest in the competence and development for our employees.

<!-- image -->

## Responsible Consumption and Production

Ambea creates modern residential facilities with lower CO₂ emissions. We minimise transport and food waste. By keeping our stocks down, we only consume as much as we need. All waste generated by our units is handled responsibly.

<!-- image -->

## Social sustainability

Social sustainability is engrained in our DNA, as an employ -er and in our units. Here we are focused on quality improvement, OHS, leadership, diversity and inclusion, where competence development is key to the achievement of our targets. Read more about Ambea as an employer on page 43.

## Diversity and inclusion

Since 2016, Ambea has been running a number of projects in Sweden to offer work experience, training and job opportunities for newly arrived residents. The aim is to find valuable employees for our units, while also promot -ing social integration. Moreover, we believe that diverse experience and perspectives are crucial to our company's long-term success. In 2022, about 30 Ukrainian refugees were employed by Vardaga and Nytida.

In Vardaga's nursing homes, the professional role of service assistants has been introduced. These employees, who work alongside care workers, have completed a short labour market training programme and help with various practical tasks such as cooking and cleaning. That frees up time for nursing assistants, enabling them to devote more time to qualified care tasks, which improves care.

Language is often the greatest barrier for newly arrived residents in Sweden. Our employees can improve their working Swedish in a simple and fun way using a language app in their mobile phones. About half of the new arrivals who have joined Ambea over the past two years have either continued to work at the company or are now studying to become a care worker. This is an excellent result, and supports our continued efforts.

Abeba began as a language trainee and is now a Team Manager. Read more on page 43.

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

## Engagement for a good life

Our basic premise is to always strive for the best life possible for our care receivers. If someone wants to swim in the sea, or eat their favourite dish in the middle of the week - we do our best to arrange it. Ambea's set of values Our World provides the basis for an organisation where everyone is moving in the same direction and creates pride in our shared mission: to support and assist our care receivers in the best way possible.

There are several work-oriented day services in Nytida, where our coaches work with care receivers in a structured manner to find work experience placements with the ultimate goal of employment in the open labour market. In 2022, about 210 people from our day services were engaged in some form of work experience.

<!-- image -->

ACCESSIBLE VOTING. In the run-up to the general election in 2022, several of Vardaga's nursing homes contacted their municipality to see whether it would be possible to set up a polling station at their home. That would enable our care receivers to vote in advance, in person, with their poll card and ID.

## Work experience through Nytida's day services led to dream job

Kasper's job journey began at one of Nytida's day services units with a focus on work experience and employment. He looked for work experience together with his coach and has now received his dream job at Tolv, a combination of restaurants and activities such as ten-pin bowling, karaoke, shuffleboard, arcade games and much more.

'I've always wanted to work in the hospitality industry,' he says.

His work experience was converted into a position that is tailored to him. He starts at ten in the morning with various preparations before the venue opens at 11.

'I open the electronic cash registers, sort the bowling shoes and vacuum the shuffleboard powder. The best part is teaching new trainees. I work alongside different colleagues every day and it works really well. We get to know each other after a few days. Now I've found the right job and I love it here,' says Kasper.

'I've always wanted to work in the hospitality industry,' he says. Now I've found the right job and I love it here.'

Kasper, employed at Tolv

We believe that exercise and physical activity are import -ant for everyone, based on their own terms. There are activity managers in all of Vardaga's residential facilities who are responsible for arranging daily activities based on the residents' requests.

## SUPPORT FOR UKRAINE

Ambea has a long tradition of supporting the community in various crises and in 2022, the focus was on the war in Ukraine, which has affected and engaged people in many ways. We made several contributions during the year:

- To reduce human suffering, both Ambea and our employees made donations to aid organisations including The Red Cross, SOS Barnbyar and UNHCR, which have operations across Ukraine and in neighbouring countries.
- We reviewed our purchasing processes and suspended all purchases from around ten suppliers of goods and services that have chosen to remain active in Russia.
- Our newly constructed nursing home Villa Öresund in Helsingborg, which is not yet in operation, is now rented to the municipality to offer accommodation for Ukrainian refugees.
- About 30 Ukrainian refugees have received employment or work experience in Vardaga and Nytida.
-  In addition, several units worked locally and arranged sports events and activities to collect clothing, food and hygiene kits that were sent to Ukraine.

<!-- image -->

## GHG emissions and control

Ambea is guided by principles based on a strong valuesbased culture, regulatory compliance and transparent dialogue. We take responsibility for the environment by adapting our operations to climate change and switching to fossil-free. We have adopted an ambitious target to halve our GHG emissions by 2025, compared with 2019. With a few years to go, we've come a long way, but aren't there yet. In this respect, partnerships with clients, authorities, suppliers, other companies and union organisations are important, as well as our own ability to innovate and a strong ambition to minimise our impact on the planet.

## Ambea's GHG emissions

Our starting point is to take responsibility for, and reduce, our direct emissions to become aligned with the Paris Agreement and net zero emissions by 2050. An overall assessment of our value chain emissions is required to allocate the right resources to the right place. Since we started compiling data in 2019, our understanding of the primary sources of our GHG emissions has increased. The primary source of our direct (Scope 1) GHG emissions is internal transportation. Heating and some electricity account for our Scope 2 emissions, while food, consumables and waste are the primary sources of our indirect (Scope 3) emissions. Most of Ambea's emissions, just over 80 per cent, are Scope 3 emissions. Our total emissions of 29,602 tonnes CO 2 e (tCO 2 e) correspond roughly to emissions from the annual consumption of 3,700 Swedes 1 , a figure that can be compared with our 16,500 care receivers. In the coming years, our efforts will be focused on fossil-free vehicle and energy solutions - including the phase-out of fossil vehicles - and on finding joint solutions together with our customers, property owners, food and consumables suppliers to reduce our GHG emissions.

Our environmental performance is monitored twice per year by carrying out self-assessments in the units. Com-

1 Swedish Environmental Protection Agency (8 tonnes of CO₂e/Swede)

<!-- image -->

## Focus on electric cars

Company cars and service cars account for a considerable amount of our GHG emissions. In 2022, as part of efforts to reduce our emissions, Ambea made a major investment in switching to electric cars. Our phaseout of fossil-fuel vehicles has thereby commenced and in 2023, we are planning to replace about 40 per cent of our fossil-fuel cars with environmentally friendly electric cars. Our target is that at least 90 percent of Ambea's approximately 1,200 business and company cars are electric cars by the end of 2025.

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

## Green annex to rental contracts

Vectura owns some of the properties that are rented by Ambea's units and during the year, Ambea and Vectura formed a partnership to create climate-smart, energy-efficient and future-proof premises. Solar panels have been installed on roofs and a green annex has been added to all rental contracts. The annex sets out a list of joint activities such as the introduction of day and night inspections of the properties to review lighting and to measure temperatures.

'Ambea has set high targets for our sustainability performance and it is positive that we have committed property owners like Vectura who share our ambitions.'

Christian Horne, Head of HR and Sustainability pliance with a range of environmental aspects, including chemical handling and waste management, is evaluated.

## Good food, as well as low GHG emissions

The amount of food waste in our units is measured twice per year. By working seasonally, with lots of root vegetables, fish and chicken, and replacing beef wherever possible, Vardaga has succeeding in reducing the GHG emissions of food served in its nursing homes. According to a report from RISE (2021:42), average GHG emissions per person and day from food consumption in Sweden is 5.2 carbon dioxide equivalents (CO 2 e). Vardaga's units generate average daily emissions of 3.2 CO 2 e per person, which is in line with the target for 2025.

All menus are adapted with consideration for the wishes of residents and in accordance with Vardaga's Food Like Homeconcept. The aim of the concept is to create a positive atmosphere around cooking and mealtimes. The menus are optimised to suit the care receivers, to help them enjoy good, varied and nutritious meals. The food is cooked or prepared on site to spread delicious smells and stimulate the appetites of the residents.

In 2023, Vardaga's efforts to reduce GHG emissions from food will be transferred to elderly care in Denmark. At the same time, we will continue working across the entire organisation to reduce the impact of food on our carbon footprint by raising awareness, making sustainable purchases and working together with our suppliers.

## ABOUT OUR emissions reduction targets

In 2022, Ambea's GHG emissions amounted to 16,870 of tCO 2 e (excl. employee commuting). Emissions were 21 per cent lower compared with the base year of 2019. We are continuously improving our data collection to achieve a full overview of our GHG emissions. At the same time, we are also gaining new insights and have also expanded the framework by including EKKO in Denmark, which was acquired 2021. Our target to halve our emissions by 2025 remains firm. Our perspectives for reductions within Scope 3 excludes employee commuting. A presentation of our GHG emissions for each Scope can be found on the next page.

*   Tonnes of CO₂ equivalents. A carbon dioxide equivalent or CO₂e is a unit of measure that is used to standardise the climate effects of various greenhouse gases over time (100 years in the Greenhouse Gas Protocol) where CO₂ has the index value of 1. The Global Warming Potential (GWP) metric is used to convert GHGs into CO₂e and nitrous oxide, for example is 273, which means that 1 kg of nitrous oxide is converted to 273 kg CO₂e. 1 Estimated emissions based on 2020 employee satisfaction survey. 2 Excluding employee commuting. 3 All available care placements during one calendar year.

Ambea calculates its GHG emissions in accordance with the Greenhouse Gas Protocol. Operational control is used as the consolidation approach, and the market-based method is used to calculate Scope 2 emissions. If Ambea had used the location-based method, Scope 2 emissions would have amounted to 2,948 tonnes of CO 2 e. The Greenhouse Gas Protocol divides emissions into reporting areas (scopes) and categories (#).

Ambea started measuring its GHG emissions in 2019. 2019 is also the base year for comparisons with other years. Due to gradual improvement of the data collection and expansion of the framework, total Scope 1, 2 and 3 emissions are not fully comparable over the years. For Denmark, EKKO's emissions have been added. For more information about definitions and calculation method, refer to page 136.

## ambea:

Annual Report with Quality &amp; Sustainability Report 2022

Our competitive environment

24

<!-- image -->

## Our competitive environment

Ambea is impacted by several competitive factors. Three clear trends at social level are creating conditions for the sector over the coming decade.

The trends are largely due to demographic shifts that will cause social pressure across all Scandinavian countries - but also create opportunities for Ambea to offer assistance and solutions. We refer to these trends as: The major welfare challenge:

- Fewer will have to support more
- Care needs are growing
- Growing shortage of care workers

## Fewer will have to support more

In the years ahead, the proportion of older people in the population will grow, but also children and young people. This trend means that relatively fewer people of working age (20-64) will have to finance welfare services via their taxes. This is a recurring pattern across the entire Nordic region. In the countries where Ambea operates, this trend will be clearest in Denmark over the next decade. According to the Swedish Ministry of Finance, Swedish municipalities and regions will have a deficit of SEK 90 billion by 2026 if they continue to deliver care with the same staff density as today, all other factors being equal.

## Care needs are growing

The population of Scandinavia is growing and the oldest age groups are growing fastest percentually. Due to longer life expectancy and a baby boom in the 1940s, the number of people aged 80+ is projected to increase by about 50 per cent between 2020 and 2030. To meet this growing need, the supply of elderly care services will have to be expanded - both nursing homes and home care services. In addition,

<!-- image -->

some of the municipal care facilities that were built 30-50 years ago are now in poor condition. In many cases, refurbishing these facilities is not financially viable.

Based on the Swedish Ministry of Finance's calculations in 2019 that Timbro updated in 2021, there will be a shortage of 418 residential facilities by 2030. Unless private providers step in, the need will be even greater. There could be a shortage of 400 nursing homes by 2026. The situation is similar in Denmark. However, long-range forecasts are always associated with a degree of uncertainty. Since 2007, for example, Swedish municipalities have been granting residential care to an increasingly lower number of people in the 65+ age group.

The number of individuals with complex diagnoses has increased in recent years. This could be mental illness combined with substance abuse problems, or specialised elderly care such as geriatric psychiatry. It is often difficult for small municipalities to offer high-quality care services for these groups with the right quality and/or at a reasonable cost.

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

## Growing shortage of care workers

Care delivery is highly labour-intensive. There is already a shortage of qualified employees and the problem will increase, since many of today's employees will soon retire. Needs are growing faster than the workforce across all of Scandinavia.

- The Norwegian Ministry of Health and Care Services estimates that 50 per cent of employment growth by 2040 will be needed in social care and according to Norwegian Association of Local and Regional Authorities 2021 Employer Monitor, approximately 45,600 more employees will already be needed by 2031.
- According to the Swedish Ministry of Finance, 48,000 more care workers will be needed in 2026 compared with 2018. This forecast is based on unchanged staff density.
- In Denmark, according to a report compiled by the National Association of Local Authorities in 2021, about 70 per cent of Danish municipalities already lack qualified

elderly care workers, and about half of all municipalities are also experiencing social care-related challenges.

- To tackle the worker shortage, the productivity of care services must be improved. For example, Statistics Norway (SSB) estimates that annual productivity growth of 0.5 per cent would reduce the need for employees by 18 per cent by 2040.

## Other trends affecting our market

## Growing quality and regulatory requirements

Quality of care has been garnering political attention since the 1990s and care providers are subject to comprehensive regulation. While overarching legislation has remained unchanged in recent years, demands from regulators and municipalities are tending to increase. The public/private care debate often brings attention to private providers, which means they have to perform better than public providers.

## Long-term effects of the pandemic

The long-term effects of the coronavirus pandemic are still difficult to determine. But there are signs that men -tal health problems are increasing and that a social care burden is mounting. For the care sector, the pace of development stalled during the pandemic, which delayed the necessary advancements and efficiency improvements.

## Political impacts

Policy makers determine the market conditions for companies and organisations. Municipalities are responsible for elderly care and social care, and private care providers are often a natural part of the solution to municipal needs. While municipal autonomy is strong, the sector is governed by national legislation. Stricter regulation and regulatory requirements are often introduced at government level as a means for controlling and steering companies. At present,

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

the regulatory requirements are higher for private providers. Read more on page 28.

## Growing focus on sustainability and climate change impacts

The earth's climate has always been changing, but unlike previous changes, human activity is responsible for the changes we are seeing now. Historically, abrupt climate changes have had serious consequences for life on earth. That is why demands are now increasing for companies and organisations to take responsibility for their impacts and reduce their emissions. Over time, this will also have operational and financial implications when activities also need to be aligned with environmental objectives and risks.

## Increased need for priorities

Municipal budgets will become increasingly strained as more needs/services are weighed against each other. That will force municipalities to prioritise and review how they purchase services.

## ACTIVE MEMBER OF SECTOR ORGANISATIONS

<!-- image -->

Ambea is an active member of sector and employer organisations in Sweden, Norway and Denmark. The Association of Private Care Providers in Sweden engages in advocacy and lobbying on issues that are important to Ambea and the sector to protect the interests of these companies and ensure fair market terms. Mark Jensen is a member of the Association's Board, and Ambea is represented in several sector committees and work groups. The Association's mission also includes spreading objective information about private social care. Klara is a member of Competence Agencies of Sweden and Klara's Managing Director Eva Domanders is a member of the board. Most of Ambea's collective agreements are concluded between the Association of Private Care Providers and the Swedish Municipal Workers' Union, Swedish Vision Union, Swedish Association of Health Professionals and Swedish Union for Professionals.

Stendi is a member of Confederation of Norwegian Enterprise for health care, welfare and upbringing (NHO Geneo) and in Denmark Altiden is a member of the Danish Chamber of Commerce. Both Stendi and Altiden have collective agreements concluded with trade unions in their respective markets.

## AMBEA'S STRATEGY FOR FUTURE-PROOF CARE

Ambea's updated strategy will equip us to meet the challenges and opportunities of the coming years. The strategy focuses on important areas that will strengthen the organisation, contribute to the business and develop care, which benefits both individuals and society.

<!-- image -->

1  Ambea's addressable market in elderly care, disability care and individual and family care. 2  Ambea's addressable market in disability care, and individual and family care.

## A large and growing market

The Scandinavian care services market is growing structurally and steadily. Legislation and local policies set the framework for our operations.

<!-- image -->

Freedom of choice is part of the Swedish welfare model and supported by an overwhelming majority of Swedes. A total of 86 per cent say it should be possible to choose where you live when you need elderly care, according to a survey conducted by the Association of Private Care Providers. The focus of the debate has shifted slightly in recent years - from calls to ban profits, to the social challenges posed by growing care needs and strained municipal budgets. 2022 was a general election year in Sweden. A new Centre-Right government came into power and new municipal constellations were formed.

## Market

The Swedish care market 1  grew almost 5 per cent annually between 2016 and 2021. Elderly care demand and occupan -cy continued to rise in 2022, following a temporary decline in 2020 due to the coronavirus pandemic, and are now in line with pre-pandemic levels. The share of private care in Sweden is around 20 per cent, with a lower share in elderly care and a higher share in individual and family care.

Total market size: SEK 232 billion Private care: 20% Ambea's position in private care: 1 Other private providers: Attendo, Humana, Team Olivia, Frösunda, Förenade Care, Norlandia

<!-- image -->

In Norway, freedom of choice in care services cannot be taken for granted. The political debate on private welfare providers is highly polarised, especially around child protection. Norway's minority government, which took office in 2021, wants a strong public sector with fewer commercial welfare providers and strengthened collaboration with non-profit players in the social care sector. At the same time, the importance of securing resources for municipalities and giving them the freedom to solve their welfare mission has been emphasised. The opposition claims that people want freedom of choice when it comes to welfare services, and that using all positive forces to solve welfare challenges will be important in the future.

## Market

The care market 2  in Norway rose 7 per cent annually during the 2017-2021 period. The privatisation rate for care in personal assistance, care services and child protection is 13 per cent.

Total market size: NOK 120 billion Private care: 13% Ambea's position in private care: 1 Other private providers: Humana, Ecura, Team Olivia, Uloba, Norlandia/Aberia

<!-- image -->

In Denmark, there is broad political consensus that freedom of choice is a key element of the country's welfare model. The public debate revolves around quality, care resources and how the growing needs for care can be solved. 2022 was a general election year in Denmark and a broad left-right coalition came into power. The new coalition government is planning a wide range of reforms including an overhaul of its welfare model, with a focus on creating more jobs, prioritising greater freedom of choice and collaboration with private providers.

## Market

The private market for Ambea's care services in Denmark is growing faster than the total market, indicating a growing privatisation rate. The market grew 5 per cent between 2018 and 2021. About 70 per cent of disability care and psychosocial support services are provided by private operators.

Other private providers: Habitus, OK Fonden, Danske

Total market size: DKK 84 billion Private care: 29% Ambea's position in private care: 1 Diakon Hjem, Attendo, SUF

We work with personalised care in all of our units. This means that every person in our units should experience quality from their own personal perspective, and thereby live the best life possible in the way they want.

## Personalised care

Quality concepts and clear frameworks are used in all of our units to ensure consistent and high levels of quality. Competence development and continuous learning ensure high quality and daily development for our employees.

The quality concepts are based on proven experience and scientific evidence. Our elderly care concept is based on knowledge in several areas that are important for high-quality elderly care. The concepts address specific features of dementia, or the individual's own wishes based on their background and conditions. Vardaga's The Good Day concept consists of several promises to care receivers and their loved ones about how we act, and what they are entitled to expect in relation to food, activities and dementia care.

Nytida has a similar concept, Pedagogical Framework, and clear processes with the aim of achieving personalised support based on the individual care receiver's strengths, needs and challenges.

The Good Day and Pedagogical Framework concepts are gradually being implemented and adapted to units in Stendi and Altiden.

## Quality based on knowledge

High quality and continuous quality improvement are fundamental for Ambea. Systematic quality management ensures satisfied care receivers, their loved ones and cli -ents, and permeates our entire organisation. Our quality management is aimed at consistently meeting regulatory requirements, as well as the expectations of individuals and loved ones.

With competence development and continuous learning, we are ensuring quality of care while also enabling our employees to develop in their professional roles on a daily

<!-- image -->

Our nursing homes under own management are designed for people with dementia, cognitive impairment or palliative care needs.

basis. Our own training company, Lära, is a Swedish lead -er in training and competence-enhancement programmes for social work, care, schools and treatment. Every year, about 7,000 of our employees take part in some type of competence-enhancement programme through Lära.

## ENVIRONMENTS ADAPTED TO NEEDS

Our nursing homes under own management are designed for people with dementia, cognitive impairment or palliative care needs. Group homes for people with neuropsychiatric disorders are designed to support the residents' needs, including very quiet environments, environments that reduce the risk of affective responses, and the need for both companionship and privacy.

<!-- image -->

## Support that strengthens individuals

Nytida is the leading disability care company in Sweden. We provide support and care for children, young people and adults with lifelong disabilities and psychosocial problems.

A t Nytida's approximately 450 units, 9,000 employees provide support and care adapted to the strengths, abilities and preferences of each care receiver. The operations are divided into three segments: disability care, individual and family care, and schools.

## Disability care

People with disabilities such as autism spectrum disorder or acquired brain injury often have a lifelong need for support and care. Due to Nytida's broad range of services, we can offer a complete spectrum of care with personalised support throughout all stages and needs of life. For chil -dren and young people, there are child care homes, holiday camps and short-term housing. Services for adults include group homes, assisted living facilities and day services.

## Individual and family care

The aim of individual and family care is to help individuals gradually develop skills for a better everyday life and even -tually become independent. The care receivers have a broad spectrum of challenges, such as substance abuse, self-harming behaviour or criminality, often combined with mental or neuropsychiatric disorders. Nytida offers personalised support in the form of HVB homes (homes for care or residence), assisted living facilities, supported living facilities, foster homes, shel -

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

tered housing, non-residential care and can also provide needs assessments for children, young people and adults.

## Schools

All children and young people have a right to attend a school where they can develop according to their own ability, be able to succeed, feel secure and be treated with respect. For children and young people with neuropsychiatric disorders, Nytida offers day-care centres, elementary schools, special needs elementary schools and special needs upper secondary schools in accordance with the Swedish Education Act. Teaching is adapt -ed to the strengths and developmental skills of each individual.

## Nytida's care: A focus on every care receiver

The support and care offered by Nytida requires employees with high levels of knowledge and competence in the estab -lished methodologies. A key starting point is personalised interventions for every care receiver. The goal is to enable as much independence as possible and quality of life.

## Pedagogical Framework

A tool for personalising support is Nytida's Pedagogical Framework concept. It is an approach and guidance that gives employees the conditions they need to understand the needs of individuals and respond to them with adequate methods and tools. The framework is based on Ambea's set of values, evidence-based practice, the low-arousal approach and person-centred care.

## Interior design concept: 'Hem'

Nytida has been working with the Home residential concept in our newly constructed assisted living facilities since 2020. The aim of Home is to create group homes that feel like home, while also creating a safe environment for our specific target groups and employees. The special features include double en -trances and exits in the apartments for greater security and extra soundproofing because our care receivers can be noise sensitive. The décor is high-contrast to facilitate and clarify the purpose of the facility. Nytida's Hem concept is based on many years of experience and scientific evidence gathered from research into our target groups.

-  Nytida provided training in sexuality and relationships for employees. The training serves as a common springboard for responding to, and supporting, Nytida's various target groups in issues related to sex, sexuality and relationships.
-  The Kung Saga special-needs upper-secondary school celebrated its tenth anniversary with a festival in the school's premises in Stockholm.

## Elderly care for quality of life

Vardaga is one of the largest private elderly care providers in Sweden, with more than 100 residential facilities across the country, and home care services in

V ardaga has about 12,000 employees who create quality of life and security for every individual care receiver, where every day should feel meaningful.

## Nursing homes

Our residential facilities provide round-the-clock social care for the elderly who can no longer stay in their homes due to illness or age-related weakness. Care placements are offered for older people with either dementia or somatic (physical) complaints. Vardaga also runs facilities with speciality placements in areas including geriatric psychia -try, and for people with young-onset dementia (under 65). In addition to permanent residential placements, Vardaga offers short-term placements, relief care and respite care. Our residential facilities are located all over Sweden, with a certain concentration to the Stockholm-Mälar Region, Skåne and Västkusten. About 60 per cent are operated under own management, and 40 per cent under contract management.

## Home care

Vardaga also offers home care with personalised services and help with everyday chores, such as cleaning, washing, shop -ping, private nursing, cooking, walks and companionship. There are 14 home care teams in total, with just over 2,500 customers. This offering is available in the Stockholm region, mainly concentrated to inner Stockholm, and Linköping.

## Vardaga's elderly care: The Good Day

At Vardaga's nursing homes, every care receiver should

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

be able to live a dignified and meaningful life based on their own preferences. The Good Day concept is applied to achieve this high quality of care, comprising a range of promises related to various aspects of care. Vardaga's special offerings include:

Active Daily Living: Our aim is that every day will be filled with quality of life and meaningful content for each individ -ual care receiver. All of Vardaga's residential facilities have at least one activity manager.

Food Like Home: Most of Vardaga's units apply the Food Like Home concept, which means that all food is prepared in the unit's own kitchen. The aim is that our care receivers will look forward to mealtimes as the highlight of their day.

Pet Therapy: Many of the nursing homes receive regular visits from therapy and pet dogs who spread joy and support exercise and medical treatment.

Young Care: Many of our homes receive appreciated visits from sociable young people on weekends and school holi -days, through a multi-year partnership with the Young Care organisation.

The Good Night: Ensuring a good night's sleep is a vital com -ponent of care. Together with each care receiver, we draw up a plan to help them sleep better at night.

Dementia Academy: The Dementia Academy is part of Lära (Ambea's training organisation), and is a centre of excellence for dementia that offers certification schemes, training and support for Vardaga's units.

End-of-life care: Vardaga is focused on training teams of rep -resentatives from various professions in the units to become palliative care officers. We want to provide the best possible end-of-life care for our residents. Trained, equipped and confident employees ensure tailored and personal end-of-life care for the residents, and positive and professional support for their loved ones.

## 2022: HIGHER OCCUPANCY AND FAVOURABLE DEMAND

-  During the year, both occupancy and demand increased in our existing units. In Vardaga's mature units, occupancy is now in line with pre-pandemic levels and occupancy increased month-by-month in our newer units.
-  The long-term need for elderly care places remains high. During the year, Vardaga opened five new homes with a total of 367 beds.
-  Vardaga also expanded its home-care operations in Stockholm by acquiring Hannas Hemtjänst.
-  Several new rental contracts were signed for Own Management operations during the year. At yearend, Vardaga had a total of eight nursing homes either contracted or under construction.
-  A new training programme for activity managers was introduced during the year. There must be at least one activity manager in every facility and the aim of the training programme is to strengthen the role and share best practices within the organisation.

## Care solutions in Norway

Stendi is the largest private care provider in Norway. The company offers a wide range of care solutions for children, young people and adults.

S tendi is Ambea's Norwegian division and offers nationwide support and residential care services for children, young people and adults. Stendi also offers personal assistance. The company has more than 300 units and some 6,500 employees across Norway. In 2021, a decision was made to divest elderly care in Stendi to focus on social care instead, where Stendi is a market leader.

## Disability and individual care

Stendi offers personalised residential facilities and care services for people with various types of complex assistance needs. One target group is people with disabilities, such as autism and cognitive impairment, where the need for social support interventions is often life long. Other target groups are people with, for example, substance abuse problems, early-onset dementia or mental health problems.

They receive care in residential facilities with staff density adapted to individual needs. For individuals with high care needs, there are assisted living facilities with on-site 24-hour staffing. Less formal types of care include home support, where care receivers remain in their own apartments and receive regular visits from Stendi's employees.

As a complement to residential care, Stendi also operates activity centres to provide each resident with a meaningful daily life in companionship with each other.

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

## Individual and family care

For children and young people, Stendi offers placements in foster homes and children's homes. The target group is children and young people with various types of problems and needs for support and care, who are unable to live at home for various reasons. As a complement, Stendi also offers services for these people when they leave the facility to support their transition to independent living. Stendi also offers short-break-only homes for children and young people with severe disabilities, as well as assistance at home for children with physical disabilities.

## Personal assistance

For children and adults with a certain type of disability, Stendi offers user-controlled personal assistance (BPA). This service means that care receivers receive assistance with activities of daily living in order to live as inde -pendently as possible and lead the lifestyle they desire.

## Elderly care

In 2022, Stendi managed three nursing homes on behalf of municipalities or city districts. In 2022, Stendi divested some of its elderly care units.

## Music therapy creates communication

Stendi uses a range of models and methods to offer high-quality care. One example is music therapy for children and young people, as well as adults. It is an evi -dence-based form of therapy based on the ability of music to create contact, communication and motivation.

## 2022: IMPROVEMENTS THAT LED TO RESULTS

-  During the year, Stendi worked intensively to adapt the capacity of both care placements and employees to demand.
-  70 unit managers completed a leadership and competence-enhancement programme that Stendi developed together with VID Specialised University.
-  Stendi began divesting elderly care in 2022 by selling its only facility under own management.
-  Start-up of a new employer branding campaign to improve the attractiveness of the sector and the operation.
-  Stendi's training platform for employees was further developed.
-  Stendi will sponsor the Kongsberg Games, an event that aims to empower children and young people with a disability through physical activity and inclusion.

## Growing business in Denmark

Altiden is the largest private care provider in Denmark with disability and elderly care services.

A ltiden operates about 55 units across Denmark, where some 2,000 employees are working every day to create quality of life, security and development for the care receivers in their units. The goal is that every per -son can live the life they want, and the support is personalised to meet each individual's needs and preferences.

## Disability and individual care

Altiden has extensive experience and specialist expertise in support and care for people with disabilities such as brain damage, autism, ADHD and cognitive impairment. The offering consist of residential facilities and home support, as well as extra support for young adults in the form of a network.

For children and young people with disabilities such as autism and attention deficit disorders, Altiden offers res -idential special schools. Here, the focus lies on the young person's strengths, not their weaknesses, and the activities are adapted to the needs of each individual.

## Social care and social advice

Altiden offers social advice and guidance for children, young people, adults and families, which includes contact persons, supported independent living services, and family support. We also provide residential care for children and

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

young people with special needs, and have a number of transitional living apartments.

## Elderly care

For the elderly, Altiden offers a wide range of care services:

- Nursing homes with a wide range of activities for a meaningful daily life, and a focus on good food and pleas -ant mealtimes.
- Day centres with get-togethers and social activities for the elderly who have moved to a nursing home, as well as those who are still living in their homes. The visitors can cook or exercise together, for example, or listen to an interesting talk.
- Rehabilitation centres to help people recover from a se -rious accident or illness. The goal is to improve physical abilities to enable a fast return to daily life.

## 2022: ACQUISITIONS AND NEW UNITS

-  During the year, Altiden acquired two social care units through Huset Reflekt, which added nine new beds, and SK Reflekt, which offers support and guidance for vulnerable children, young people and adults.
-  Altiden also took over the operation of Lindehaven, a newly opened nursing home with beds for 125 care receivers.
-  Altiden's new Managing Director, Jan Wilken, took office on 1 November.

## Klara secures competence

Klara is a leading Swedish provider of specialised solutions for social care with a focus on staffing, mobile healthcare teams and student health services.

K lara is an authorised and ISO-certified competence company that supplies thousands of temporary staff every year. Klara is thereby helping to ensure that urgent procedures can be carried out, waiting lists can be shortened, nursing homes and wards can offer safe and effective care, and that students are offered health services by our medical staff.

To meet the challenges of welfare and strained social care budgets, Klara will continue to develop team solutions with the aim of offering sustainable solutions where fewer can deliver care to more, both young and old.

With personal service and long experience in the sector, we offer value-generating competence provision to both public and private clients. Customers are mainly municipalities, regions, private social care providers and indepen -dent schools.

## Klara's mobile healthcare-teams - a cost-efficient solution

Klara's mobile healthcare teams offer nurses for on-call and standby duty during day, evening and night shifts at nursing homes. Mobile nursing teams are also available for day shifts at assisted living facilities. The service is offered on a subscription basis. A key customer category is small facilities that may find it difficult to maintain sufficient staffing capacity. Klara Rehab and its teams can also provide occupational therapists, and physiotherapists

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

provide consultation for personalised nursing homes and LSS services, and hold training courses.

## Student health services

Student health services in Klara works with more than 200 schools and can assume provider responsibility for the medical treatment offered by student health services. During the year, Klara's student health services launched online solutions, where smaller schools are offered online medical services, which increases availability and is also cost-efficient.

Klara also offers staffing solutions for all of student health services professional categories, such as doctors, nurses and social workers, who are outsourced for both long-term and short-term positions.

## Staffing

Klara is a certified competence company that supplies temporary nursing staff and social workers. The services include both long-term and short-term solutions. Klara's nurses work in care or somatic care under staffing con -tracts or as self-employed. With its 3,000 nurses, Klara has a large database, which is crucial for finding the right person for every position.

## Complement to Ambea's core business operations

In addition to placements for external clients, Klara also supports Ambea's core business operations. Klara provides qualitative and cost-efficient on-call solutions when regular employees are off duty, as well as rehabilitation staff for Vardaga and Nytida, and a recruitment pool for nurses and managers.

## 2022: FOCUS AND DEVELOPMENT

-  During the year, Klara acquired SkolPool. The acquisition created a unique market position for Klara in student health, where there is a growing need for a well-developed service.
-  Klara divested its staffing solutions for doctors to focus on service areas that are more in line with Ambea's other activities.
-  Our mobile team solutions in Klara, such as rehab and day time mobile teams, performed well during the year and can now be found in more locations where Klara has existing operations.

## Competence and knowledge hub

Lära is a Swedish leader in training and competenceenhancement programmes in social work, care, schools and treatment. Lära is one of Ambea's brands, but is not recognised as a separate division.

L ära operates across Sweden and offers a wide range of training solutions in the form of in-person, online or short e-learning courses. Lära also provides tailored solutions to customer needs, and supervision for solving specific challenges.

Lära trains and supervises about 13,000 people every year, including 7,000 Ambea employees.

Lära offers courses in:

- the low-arousal approach
- motivational conversation
- person-centred care
- pedagogical approaches
- mental illness
- cognitive impairment
- clear communication
- leadership.

<!-- image -->

-  At the end of 2021, Lära formed a partnership with the Danish founders of Kat-Kit. The Kat-kit is a toolbox that provides visual support for people with neuropsychiatric disabilities to, for example, develop the ability to communicate with others. ## Our employees

Every day, our thousands of employees are helping and supporting the people in our units. Our vision is to make the world a better place, one person at a time. Hands-on managers, continuous competence development and a strong set of values are key factors.

Finding and retaining competent employees is crucial to Ambea's ability to continue providing high-quality social care. The underlying principle is to be an attractive employ -er by offering good working conditions and a pleasant work environment. Ambea also offers good development and ca -reer opportunities, as well as coaching and supportive lead -ership for care workers. We engage actively with several schools in areas such as work experience. Ambea wants to be the first choice for students, and the aim of our model for school and work experience coordination in our regions is that students in relevant training programmes will account for a growing proportion of our recruitment pool.

## Career with Ambea

Ambea is a major employer with many opportunities for employees to develop, make a career or switch roles within the Group.

- We strive to recruit at least seven of ten managers inter -nally. That enables us to harness valuable organisational competence and ensures smooth transitions whenever new managers start.
- There are many roles in our units that require expertise, including scheduling, activities, diet and dementia, which gives our employees an opportunity to take responsibility for a specific area. They receive extra training and can then provide support for their colleagues and manager in their area of expertise.

## Journey from work experience to team manager

Abeba Fisshaye's journey in Vardaga began seven years ago, when she took part in a language trainee programme at one of Vardaga's nursing homes. She had come to Sweden from Eritrea two years earlier and the home offered placements for several trainees with backgrounds in various countries to help them improve their skills in both elderly care and Swedish. The aim was to attract employees to Vardaga.

'At the end of my language traineeship, I was offered employment on an hourly basis, followed by a temporary position and finally permanent em -ployment as a care assistant. Alongside of working, I completed elementary school and then started a nursing assistant course,' says Abeba Fisshaye.

Her Vardaga journey continued, and Abeba accom -panied her Care Manager when she transferred to the newly built Villa Orrholmen. She continued her nursing assistant studies and received her certificate in 2021. She is now Team Manager for a dementia unit with about ten employees and 20 care receivers.

'Vardaga's support is invaluable. The best place to learn a language, and about culture and work is in the workplace with colleagues,' says Abeba.

## Tailored leadership development and ability to build influence in the workplace

A distinctive feature of Ambea's leadership is hands-on managers who are engaged in the day-to-day activities of their employees. Team managers spend most of their day working alongside of our employees. This means that em -ployees can receive fast support and feedback from their manager - a model that is highly appreciated by both employees and managers. This style of leadership creates motivated and engaged employees.

All managers in the Group undergo regular training in active and values-based leadership, which has its starting point in Ambea's set of values Our World . Our tailored leadership development model creates a shared view of leadership amongst our managers. The model is based on a structured introduction with both physical and online training, and is an important tool for engaging employees and allowing them to grow. During the year, our leader -ship programme received a Gold Medal for Best advance in leadership development from Brandon Hall Group, a research and analyst firm.

## Follow up with Leadership Index

Twice per year, we ask all employees questions about their line manager, and their answers are anonymous. The aim is to ensure that the style of leadership that Ambea strives for is also delivered. The questions we ask our employees are mainly related to their manager's commitment, ability to provide support and coaching, and ability to structure work processes. The results add up to Ambea's total Lead -ership Index score, with a target of 80 out of 100, but the most important factor is that every individual manager is receptive to the feedback and develops their leadership. In 2022, the total Leadership Index score was 77.

## Continuous evaluation questionnaire

Our employees have good opportunities to influence their workplace and working conditions. Our regular employ -ee survey, the Team Barometer, is an important tool for measuring our systematic approach to health and safety, and ensures compliance with the Organisational and

## Competence development with a wow effect

Vardaga's Dementia Academy concept supports our units with training and guidance for employees, and training and support for loved ones. In 2022, just over 500 employees completed training in Vardaga's Dementia Academy, which will also be celebrating its tenth anniversary in 2023.

The programme is based on national guidelines, evi -dence-based practice and proven experience in dementia care. A major focus is how to communicate with a person with dementia. Due to the large number of nursing assis -tants who work in nursing homes, the Dementia Academy's courses are mainly designed for the professional role of nursing assistant. Helena Rödén is an instructor at Lära:

'I have had employees, both nursing assistants and nurses, who have been working for many years but say that our training gave them more than they thought. A great deal of what we teach is not included in nursing assistant programmes. When the concept is launched in a unit, all employees undergo training at the same

'Employees appreciate the clear everyday tools that can be used to communicate with the residents.'

Helena Rödén, instructor at Lära time - care managers, nurses and caretakers - so you study together with your closest colleagues. That can create a real wow effect.

After the course, a dementia officer is appointed to ensure compliance with the new knowledge. The units that complete the training become certified. A total of 44 residential facilities in Vardaga have now been certified, and just over 200 dementia officers.
